CHINA EVERBRIGHT BANK CO., LTD.

NOTES TO THE FINANCIAL STATEMENTS FOR THE YEAR ENDED 31 DECEMBER 2003

(All amounts are stated in Rmb '000 Yuan unless otherwise stated)

[English Translation for Reference only]

##### GENERAL INFORMATION AND PRINCIPAL ACTIVITIES

China Everbright Bank Co., Ltd. (the "Bank") was established in Beijing, the People's Republic of China (the "PRC") on 18 August 1992 as a financial institution and was a wholly owned subsidiary of China Everbright Group Ltd. ("China Everbright"). The original registered and paid-in capital of the Bank was Rmb1.5 billion and Rmb775 million respectively.

Pursuant to the notice issued by the People's Bank of China (the "PBOC") (Yin Fu [1995] No. 70) "Approval for the restructuring of China Everbright Bank" and the PBOC's notice (Yin Guan [1997] No. 8) "Approval for shareholders of China Everbright Bank", the Bank completed its restructuring and capital expansion on 15 January 1997 (the "Restructure"). The Bank became a joint stock commercial bank and its registered and paid-in capital were increased to Rmb2.8 billion. After the Restructure, the total number of shares issued by the Bank amounted to 2.8 billion. In August 1997, China Everbright transferred its 560 million shares of the Bank to China Everbright Limited, whose shares are listed on the Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited.

Pursuant to the shareholders' resolution and the approval from the PBOC (Yin Fu [1999] No. 278) in 1999, the registered and paid-in capital of the Bank was increased from Rmb2.8 billion to Rmb4,312 million by capitalising capital reserve of Rmb1,512 million. 1,512 million new shares of Rmb1 each were issued to the then existing shareholders (54 new shares were issued for every 100 existing shares). Arthur Andersen · Hua Qiang Certified Public Accountants verified the capital injection and issued the capital verification report on 22 September 2000.

Pursuant to the shareholders' resolution and the approval from the PBOC (Yin Fu [2000] No. 217) in 2000, the Bank issued 1.842 billion common shares of Rmb1 each at a price of Rmb1.95 on 17 October 2000. New shares were to be issued to original shareholders and were to be subscribed in cash. New shares would be issued to new investors if original shareholders waived their rights to subscription. Due to plentitude of the subscription amount, the Bank applied to the PBOC for the issuance of additional common shares. Pursuant to the notice issued by the PBOC (Yin Fu [2001] No. 147) "Approval for the Additional Issuance of Common Shares of China Everbright Bank Co., Ltd.", the registered capital of the Bank was increased from Rmb4,312 million to Rmb7,469.9 million. PricewaterhouseCoopers Zhong Tian CPAs Limited Company verified the capital injection and issued the capital verification report on 2 June 2003.

Pursuant to the shareholders' resolution on 30 April 2002 and the approval from the PBOC (Yin Fu [2002] No. 119), the registered and paid-in capital of the Bank was increased from Rmb7,469.9 million to Rmb8,216.89 million by capitalising capital surplus of Rmb746.99 million. 746.99 million new shares of Rmb1 each were issued to the then existing shareholders (1 new share was issued for every 10 existing shares). Shareholding registration work had been completed by 30 May 2002. PricewaterhouseCoopers Zhong Tian CPAs Limited Company verified the capital injection and issued the capital verification report dated 3 June 2003. The shareholding structure of the Bank before and after the issuance of the new shares was as follows:


<table border=1 style='margin: auto; word-wrap: break-word;'><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Name of shareholders</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Number of shares before the issues</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Share ownership before the new issues</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Number of shares after the new issues</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Share ownership after the new issues</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>China Everbright</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>1,804,594,407</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>24.16%</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>1,985,053,846</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>24.16%</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>China Everbright Limited</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>1,597,801,117</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>21.39%</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>1,757,581,229</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>21.39%</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Other shareholders</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>4,067,504,476</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>54.45%</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>4,474,254,925</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>54.45%</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'></td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>7,469,900,000</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>100.00%</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>8,216,890,000</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>100.00%</td></tr></table>

As approved by the PBOC, the Bank and China Development Bank (“CDB”) jointly signed “Agreement between China Development Bank and China Everbright Bank for the Transfer of Assets, Liabilities and Banking Offices of Former China Investment Bank” (the “Agreement”) on 18 March 1999. According to the Agreement, CDB transferred the assets, liabilities, equity and 137 banking offices of the former China Investment Bank (“former CIB”) to the Bank. The Agreement became effective on 18 March 1999. Pursuant to the Agreement, the Bank recorded the transferred assets and liabilities at the carrying amounts as recorded by former CIB at the effective date of the Agreement. Pursuant to the approval from the Ministry of Finance (“MOF”) of the PRC in 2001, the Bank reassessed the fair value of the transferred assets and liabilities. Goodwill of Rmb6.537 billion arising from the excess of the purchase consideration over the fair values of the identifiable assets and liabilities was recognised (Note V.21).

The principal activities of the Bank are deposit-taking, granting of transferred loans, provision of on-shore and offshore settlement services, bills discounting, issuance of financial bonds and securities, acting as agent on issuance, redemption and underwriting of government bonds, purchase and sale of government bonds, funds takings and placements, purchase and sale of foreign currencies, provision of letters of credit and guarantee services, acting as agent on collection and payment, acting as insurance agent, provision of safety deposit box service and other activities as approved by the PBOC and the State Administration of Foreign Exchange.

## II SIGNIFICANT CHANGES IN ACCOUNTING POLICIES

### Statutory surplus reserve and statutory public welfare fund

The Bank changes its recording period of provision for statutory surplus reserve and statutory public welfare fund from the period when the relevant approval from the Board of Directors is made to the period to which such provision belongs. The Bank has made retrospective adjustments in this regard, resulting in a decrease in the beginning undistributed profits for 2002 by Rmb35.21 million, an increase in statutory surplus reserve for 2002 by Rmb40.737 million, and a decrease in the beginning undistributed profits for 2003 by Rmb40.737 million (Note V. 37).

### 2 Entrusted deposits and entrusted loans

In 2003, the Bank changes the presentation of entrusted deposits and loans from balance sheet items to off-balance sheet items.

##### BASIS OF PREPARATION

The financial statements are prepared based on the Accounting Standards for Business Enterprises promulgated by the MOF of the PRC, the Accounting System for Financial Institutions and Financial System for Financial and Insurance Enterprises promulgated in 1993 by the MOF of the PRC, as well as other regulations and circulars applicable to the Bank which were issued by the MOF, the PBOC, the China Banking Regulatory Commission ("CBRC") and other relevant authorities. Please refer to respective sections in the notes to the financial statements for the relevant regulations.

The Bank also follows “Provisional Measures on Information Disclosure of Commercial Banks” promulgated by the PBOC in 2002 in preparing the notes to the financial statements.

The financial statements are combined financial statements and are prepared based on the individual financial statements and other financial data of the headquarters, branches and sub-branches. Material inter-branch transactions and balances are eliminated upon combination.

## IV PRINCIPAL ACCOUNTING POLICIES

## 1 Accounting period

The Bank’s accounting year starts on 1 January and ends on 31 December.

### 2 Reporting currency

The Bank uses Renminbi ("Rmb") as reporting currency and all amounts in the financial statements are stated in Rmb '000.

### 3 Basis of accounting and measurement principles

Unless otherwise stated, the Bank’s basis of accounting is the accrual method, and all accounts are recorded using the historical cost convention.

### 4 Foreign currency translation

The bank maintains a multi-currency bookkeeping system.

During the year end closing of accounts, all balances in foreign currency accounting books are translated into Rmb at the exchange rates stipulated by the PBOC on the closing day and combined with the balances in the Rmb books. All items in the balance sheet and profit and loss account are translated using the exchange rates stipulated by the PBOC or the cross exchange rate approved by the government at the balance sheet date. Exchange differences arising from these translations are taken to the profit and loss account. All equity accounts, apart from the undistributed profits, are translated into Rmb using the exchange rates when the transactions took place. Undistributed profits are derived according to the translated and accumulated net results in the profit and loss account.

Translation differences arising from the difference in exchange rates between the contribution dates and reporting date are recorded in the “capital surplus” account.

### 5 Cash and cash equivalents

Cash refers to all cash in hand and call deposits. Cash equivalents refer to the short-term (holding period of less than 90 days), highly-liquid assets that are readily convertible to known amounts of cash and which are subject to an insignificant risk of changes in value. Cash equivalents include other deposits placed with the PBOC, demand deposits and time deposits with a tenor less than 90 days and other monetary assets.

### 6 Provision for doubtful accounts

The Bank adopts the allowance method for doubtful accounts.

Provisions for doubtful accounts is recorded in the profit and loss account for the current period. For assets where there is evidence that they cannot be recovered, the balances are written off against the provision and where the amount written off exceeds the amount of provision already made, the difference should be directly charged to the profit and loss account for the current period. Any subsequent recoveries of doubtful accounts written off shall be credited to provision for doubtful accounts when collected.

### 7 Accounts receivable

The accounts receivable represents interest receivable of the Bank.

The Bank provides provision for interest receivable, other than that related to loans, in accordance with the notice issued by the MOF (Cai Jin [2001] No. 127) “Notice on Administrative Policy for the Making Provision and Write-off of Doubtful Accounts” (Note IV. 10).

### 8 Discounted bills

Discounted bills mainly include discount for bills of commercial customers which have been accepted but not yet mature. For the purpose of treasury management of the Bank, discounted bills also include rediscount of commercial customers bills to the PBOC and other banks and vice versa.

The discount is calculated based on the maturity value of the bill. The difference between the value of discounted bills at maturity and the amount paid for discounting is recognised as interest on bills and recorded in the profit and loss account for the current period.

The Bank provides provision for discounted commercial bills in accordance with the notice issued by the MOF (Cai Jin [2001] No. 127) “Notice on Administrative Policy for the Making Provision and Write-off of Doubtful Accounts” (Note IV. 10).

#### (a) Short-term, medium-term and long-term loans

Loans are classified by maturity. Those loans due within 1 year (including 1 year) are classified as short-term loans. Those loans due in more than 1 year are classified as medium-term and long-term loans. The portions of medium and long-term loans maturing within 1 year are classified as short-term loans.

### (b) Overdue loans

Overdue loans refer to any loans whose borrowers fail to repay by the due date (including the extension period); the passive advance resulted from the inadequate deposit balance of the acceptor, or the discounting customer when the acceptor fails to pay discounted bill; or advances resulted from off-balance sheet businesses such as letters of credit and letters of guarantee. These advances are treated as overdue loans from the date the advances are made.

### (c) Stagnant loans

Stagnant loans refer to overdue loans that have been overdue for more than 90 days.

##### (d) Doubtful loans

The following lists out the basis on which doubtful loans are classified:

(1) The borrower is bankrupt, closed or dismissed and the loan cannot be recovered after debt collection;

(2) The borrower is dead or is declared to be dead or missing according to the civil laws of the PRC while the loan cannot be recovered from the liquidation of its asset or legacy;

(3) The loan cannot be paid wholly or in part, or the loan cannot be recovered considering the insurance compensation, if any, as a result of the borrower being suffered from major natural disaster or accident;

(4) The borrower and the guarantor have completely stopped their operating activities or their business licenses have been withdrawn notwithstanding that they are not bankrupt or their operating activities have not been ceased, and the loans granted thereto cannot be recovered after taking legal procedures;

(5) The borrower is punished for offending the law. Its assets cannot meet its obligation and there are no other obligation undertakers. The loan cannot be recovered even after debt collection;

(6) Those loans for which the Bank resorts to law when the borrowers and guarantors are unable to repay when they are due or when borrowers and guarantors have no properties for the Bank to carry out the court enforcement for repayment;

(7) When the borrower is unable to repay the loans for the reasons of (1) to (6) above, the portion unable to be recovered from the foreclosed collaterals after taking into consideration the expenses required to be incurred for the foreclosure;

(8) When the borrowers and guarantors are unable to repay in relation to letters of credit, accepted bills and letter of guarantee, etc, for the reasons of (1) to (7) above;

(9) Net losses borne by the Bank due to forgery, imposture or fraudulence of bank cards;

(10) The amount of student loans that cannot be recovered after disposal of relevant collaterals and pledges and collection from the guarantors within the recourse period;

(11) The loans specifically approved to be written off by the State Council.

##### Provision for doubtful accounts

The Bank adopts the allowance method for doubtful accounts.

Pursuant to the notice issued by the MOF (Cai Jin [2001] No.127) “Notice on Administrative Policy for the Making Provision and Write-off of Doubtful Accounts” effective from 1 January 2001, a uniform policy on provision for doubtful accounts was required to be in place while provision for doubtful receivables and provision for investment losses should no longer be made. At the same time, the direct application to write off doubtful receivables and investment losses should be ceased. The balance of provision for loan losses, doubtful receivables or investment losses as at 31 December 2000 should be transferred to a combined provision account for doubtful accounts.

Provision is made according to the different levels of risks the assets are exposed to. The provision for doubtful accounts should range from 1% to 100% of the year-end balance of the financial assets.

Pursuant to the notice (Cai Jin [2001] No. 127) mentioned above, losses occurred before 31 December 2000 should be fully provided progressively within a period not exceeding 5 years from 2001. Accordingly, the Bank will adopt and observe the guidance issued by the PBOC (Yin Fa [2002] No. 98) “Guidance on the Provision for Bank Loan Losses” in making provision for loan losses based on the 5-grade classification of loans by the end of 2005.

Provision for doubtful accounts refers to provision for loans, discounted bills, placements, deposits with banks, trade bills, items bought under resale agreements, interest receivables other than those arising from loans, short-term and long-term investments other than government bonds, non-performing assets, finance lease receivable and foreclosed assets etc.

## 11 Short-term investments

Short-term investments represent government bonds and financial bonds held by the Bank. Short-term investments are initially recorded at cost, which is the balance of the total amounts paid after deducting interest due to be collected. The interest received during the holding period directly reduces the carrying value of investment except for the portion already recorded in interest receivables. Investment gain and loss is recognised upon disposal or maturity.

The Bank provides provision for short-term investments other than government bonds in accordance with the notice issued by the MOF (Cai Jin [2001] No. 127) “Notice on Administrative Policy for the Making Provision and Write-off of Doubtful Accounts” (Note IV. 10).

##### Long-term investments

Long-term investments include long-term debt investments, interest receivable from long-term investments, and other long-term investments.

Long-term debt investments are recorded at cost when they were acquired. Cost refers to the total price paid on acquisition net of incidental expenses, such as commission to brokers, taxes and handling charges, and interest receivables for the period from issuance date or the previous interest payment date to the acquisition date of the investment. The difference between the initial cost and the par value of bonds is treated as premium or discount on investment in bonds and is amortised using straight-line method over the period from the acquisition date to the maturity date. For debt investments where the principal and interest are to be repaid upon maturity in total, the accrued interest is recorded in the “interest receivable from long-term investments” account. For debt investments where the interest is paid by installments, the accrued interest is accounted for separately in the “accounts receivable” account.

Other long-term investments are equity or fund investments of the Bank on which the Bank does not have control or joint control, or ability to exercise significant influence. Long-term equity investment costs are recorded at amounts actually paid for the investment. Long-term equity investments are accounted for using the cost method of accounting and investment income is recognised when the investees declare distribution of dividends.

The Bank provides provision for long-term investments other than government bonds in accordance with the notice issued by the MOF (Cai Jin [2001] No.127) "Notice on Administrative Policy for the Making Provision and Write-off of Doubtful Accounts" (Note IV. 10).

## 13 Fixed assets and depreciation

Fixed assets refer to buildings, plant and other equipment related to the production and operation of the Bank that are held for use in the production or supply of goods or services, for rental to others, or for other administrative purposes. They have useful lives of more than 1 year and have relatively high unit price.

Fixed assets are initially recorded at cost when purchased or constructed by the Bank. Depreciation of fixed assets is calculated on the straight-line method to write off the cost of each asset to their residual values, which is 3% of the cost, over their estimated useful lives.

The classification of fixed assets and their estimated useful lives are as follows:


<table border=1 style='margin: auto; word-wrap: break-word;'><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Buildings and plant</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>5 - 35 years</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Motor vehicles</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>4 - 6 years</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Furniture, fixtures and equipment</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>3 - 10 years</td></tr></table>

A provision for impairment would be made for a fixed asset in full when the fixed assets can no longer bring in future economic benefits to the Bank. A provision for impairment for the difference between the carrying value of a fixed asset and its recoverable amount would be made when its ability to bring in future economic benefits to the Bank is suffered from significant adverse impacts.

The recoverable amount refers to the higher of the net selling price and the value in use. The net selling price refers to the amount to be received from the disposal of such asset after deducting selling expenses in an arms-length transaction as agreed by all knowledgeable transacting parties. Value in use refers to the estimated net present value of the cash flows from the continuing usage and disposal of the assets.

When all or part of the conditions for impairment loss occurred in prior periods may no longer exist, the Bank writes

back the amount estimated to be recovered in the impairment loss account.

#### Construction in progress

Construction in progress represents fixed assets under construction or being installed and is stated at cost. Cost comprises construction and other direct costs, original cost of plant and equipment, installation, including interest costs on specific borrowings used to finance the assets incurred before the assets reaching their expected usable condition. Construction in progress is transferred to the fixed assets account when the asset has reached the expected usable condition.

#### Goodwill

The excess of the cost of an acquisition over the Bank’s interest in the fair values of the net identifiable assets acquired as at the date of the transaction is recorded as goodwill and recognised as an asset on the balance sheet. Goodwill is carried at cost less accumulated amortisation and accumulated impairment losses. Goodwill is amortised on the straight-line basis over 20 years.

### Long-term prepaid expenses and amortisation

Long-term prepaid expenses refer to long-term prepayments that should be amortised for more than 1 year.

Long-term prepaid expenses mainly include renovation costs for operational buildings under operating lease, rental prepayment and other capital expenditures. Long-term prepaid expenses also include expenses incurred during the pre-operating period for pre-operating branches and sub-branches.

Renovation cost for buildings under operating lease are amortised on the straight-line method over the shorter of the lease period and the estimated useful life.

All expenses incurred during the pre-operating period of pre-operating branches and sub-branches are first recorded as long-term prepaid expenses, and are then charged in full to the profit and loss in the first month the branch or sub-branch commences commercial operations.

## 17 Foreclosed assets

Foreclosed assets represent the collaterals received from borrowers as compensation for outstanding principals and/or interest receivables of loans based on mutual agreement or judgement of the courts when the borrowers were unable to repay in cash. Upon the transfer of the ownership of such collaterals, they are recorded at the lower of the total of outstanding principals and interest receivables of loans, and the value stipulated by the court, the value agreed by both parties or the value estimated by qualified valuers. Such collaterals include buildings, land, machinery, vehicles and equity shares.

Since foreclosed assets are resulted from default in loan repayment and are not held by the Bank for use in its production or supply of goods or services, rental to others, or other administrative purposes, the Bank does not manage and account for the foreclosed assets as fixed assets. The Bank provides provision for foreclosed assets in accordance with the notice issued by the MOF (Cai Jin [2001] No. 127) “Notice on Administrative Policy for the Making Provision and Write-Off of Doubtful Accounts” (Note IV. 10). The gain and loss arising from the disposal of foreclosed assets is recognised in the profit and loss account.

Interests on loans (including placements with banks and financial institutions), are recognised as income periodically in accordance with the contractual interest rates.

Unless otherwise stated in the principal accounting policies, other income is recognised on an accrual basis.

Pursuant to the notice issued by the MOF (Cai Jin [2002] No. 5) “Notice on Shortening the Accounting Term for Interest Receivables”:

For interest receivables from loans that are overdue within 90 days (including 90 days), interest income should be continued to be recognised. For interest receivables that are overdue for more than 90 days (excluding 90 days), regardless of the overdue status of the corresponding loan principal, recognition of interest income should be ceased and the related interest receivable should be recorded in an off-balance sheet account and be recognised as income upon actual receipt.

Pursuant to the notice issued by the MOF (Cai Jin [2001] No. 25) “Announcement Regarding Revision of Policies on Recognition of Interest Receivables”:

When loan principal or its interest receivables become overdue for more than 90 days (excluding 90 days), previously recognised but still uncollected interest receivables should be reversed in the interest income account.

For uncollected interest receivables recognised before 31 December 2000, the financial institutions should reverse them in the interest income account gradually within a period not exceeding 5 years. Financial institutions should no longer make provision for interest receivables and cease the adoption of write-off policy in this regard. The Bank has made a plan to fully write off the relevant uncollected interest receivables before 2005.

Compound interest on interest receivables should not be recognised in the profit and loss account. They should be recorded in an off-balance sheet account and recognised as income upon actual receipt.

## 19 Expense recognition

Interest expenses on demand deposits are settled quarterly. Interests on time deposits, demand and time savings deposits are accrued quarterly in accordance with the deposit amount, and the interest rate stipulated in the deposit certificate or the statutory deposit rate.

Unless otherwise stated in the principal accounting policies, other expenses are accounted on an accrual basis.

Items bought under resale agreements and items sold under repurchase agreements

Items bought under resale agreements and items sold under repurchase agreements are stated at historical cost. The income and expenses are recognised on an accrual basis.

Items bought under resale agreements or sold under repurchase agreements include loans, bonds and discounted bills.

##### Income tax

The Bank provides for income tax on the basis of its total profit for financial reporting purposes, adjusted for items which are not assessable or deductible for income tax purposes. Pursuant to the notice (Guo Shui Han [1996] No. 364) issued by the State Administration of Taxation, the Bank provides income tax at a rate of 33%.

The Bank recognised the excess of the purchase cost over the fair value of the identifiable assets and liabilities acquired, which is mainly resulted from the provision made for doubtful assets of the former CIB, as goodwill.

Pursuant to the notice issued by the MOF and the State Administration of Taxation (Cai Shui [2002] No. 198) "Notice on Tax Issues regarding Write-Off and Disposal of Non-performing Assets of China Everbright Bank" (Note V. 45), the balance of provision for doubtful loans is deductible for taxation purpose when the Bank writes off these doubtful loans in subsequent years. Thus, there is a significant deductible temporary difference. Income tax is accounted for using the tax payable method, except for the recognition of the above-mentioned deductible temporary difference for which tax effect accounting method is adopted.

## 22 Business tax and surcharges

Business tax and surcharges are paid to the respective local tax bureaus by the individual branches of the Bank. Pursuant to the notice issued by the MOF and the State Administration of Taxation (Cai Shui [2001] No. 21) “Notice on Reduction of Business Tax Rate for Financial and Insurance Companies”, business tax is provided at 5% (2002: 6%) of total operating income after deducting interest income from financial institutions and government bonds since 1 January 2003.

## 23 Derivative financial instruments

Major derivatives include foreign currency forwards, foreign currency swaps and interest rate swaps. These transactions are conducted to reduce market risks arising from certain transactions with customers. The Bank accounts for the unsettled derivative transactions as off-balance sheet items. The unrealised gain or loss resulted from the currency forwards and swaps at the balance sheet date is recognised in the profit and loss account of the period. The gain or loss resulted from the interest swaps is recognised in the profit and loss account of the period when the contracts settled at maturity.

## 24 Changes in accounting policy

A change in accounting policy should be made only if required by statute, by an accounting standard setting body, or if the change will result in a more appropriate presentation of events or transactions in the financial statements of the Bank.

A change in accounting policy should be applied retrospectively unless the amount of any resulting adjustment that relates to prior years cannot be reasonably determined and, in which case, the change in accounting policy would be applied prospectively.

## 25 Staff welfare and retirement benefit

The Bank participates in social security system which includes retirement and medical insurance, housing fund and other social security systems. According to the relevant regulations, insurance premium and housing fund is provided based on certain percentage of the employees' total salary and paid to labor and social security authorities. Such expenses are charged to the operating expenses in the current period.

#### Related parties

A related company is a company which can directly or indirectly control the Bank or exercise significantly influence to the Bank’s financial and operating policies. A related company also includes a company under common control or common significant influence of the Bank.

#### Operating leases

Leases where substantially all the risk and rewards of ownership of the assets remain with the lessors are accounted for as operating leases. Rental payments under operating lease are charged to the profit and loss account on the straight-line basis over the period of the relevant leases.

(b) On 18 March 1999, the effective acquisition date of the former CIB, included in the assets and liabilities acquired from the former CIB were World Bank entrusted loans and related interest receivables (“World Bank entrusted loans”) of US$852 million (equivalent to Rmb7.056 billion) and related borrowings of US$586 million (equivalent to Rmb4.852 billion) undertaken by the former CIB. The difference between the entrusted loans and related borrowings amounting to US$266 million (equivalent to Rmb2.204 billion) was resulted from the repayment of the loans by the former CIB with its own funds when the loans became due.

On 18 August 2000, the MOF issued the notice (Cai Jin [2000] No. 61) “Response Regarding the Granting of Supportive Financial Policies to China Everbright Group Ltd.”, allowing the Bank to treat the outstanding entrusted loans of US$853 million as off-balance sheet items. In addition, the MOF required the Bank to set up specific accounts for the recovery of the World Bank entrusted loans and to pay any proceeds recovered to the MOF.

In accordance with the above notice, the Bank has spun off outstanding World Bank entrusted loans of Rmb7.056 billion and related borrowings of Rmb4.852 billion and recorded the difference of Rmb2.204 billion as “Other receivables”.

Future cash receipts from World Bank entrusted loans are first recorded as other payables and would be used to offset the other receivables upon approval of the MOF.

### Fixed assets pending disposal

Fixed assets pending disposal mainly comprise balances relating to housing funds. Pursuant to the notice issued by the MOF (Cai Jin [2001] No. 28) "Notice Regarding Reform of Housing System for Financial Institutions", the housing fund management system was terminated with effect from 1 January 2001 and the housing fund account should no longer be used. If the remaining balance is a negative amount, the total amount should be separately accounted for under the caption of "fixed assets pending disposal". The remaining balance in the account can be written-off as non-operating expenses upon approval by the relevant governmental departments; otherwise, the remaining amount should be amortised within 5 years.

During the year, the charge to non-operating expenses arising from the amortisation of housing fund amounted to Rmb103 million (2002: Rmb103 million).

The origination of goodwill is as follows:

When the former CIB was acquired on 18 March 1999, the carrying amount of the net assets of the former CIB amounted to Rmb2.759 billion, which was subsequently adjusted to Rmb3.476 billion in 2000 arising from the exchange gain of Rmb717 million upon revaluing the working capital denominated in foreign currencies of the former CIB. As at 31 December 2002, pursuant to the notice issued by the MOF (Cai Kuai Zi [1997] No. 30) “Provisional Regulations on Accounting Treatments for Company Merger”, the amount was recorded in the “capital surplus” account. Because of the poor quality of the assets acquired from the former CIB, differences existed between the carrying amounts of the net assets and the fair values thereof. In 2001, in accordance with the “MOF’s Considerations on Recognition of Goodwill in China Everbright Bank’s acquisition of former China Investment Bank” (Cai Jin [2001] No. 20) issued by the MOF, the Bank reassessed the transferred assets and liabilities at fair values at the effective date of the Agreement and recognised the excess of the purchase cost over the fair values of the identifiable assets and liabilities transferred as goodwill. The purchase consideration was zero and thus goodwill represents fair value of the net assets less purchase consideration, taking into account the impact of deferred taxation. The calculation is as follows:


<table border=1 style='margin: auto; word-wrap: break-word;'><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Carrying amount of the net assets from former CIB</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>2,759,425</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Exchange gain arising from revaluation of working capital denominated in foreign currencies</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>717,072</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Subtotal</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>3,476,497</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Provision for doubtful accounts according to fair value</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>(14,779,172)</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Deferred tax debits arising from provisions made</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>4,765,940</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Adjusted net liabilities (i.e. goodwill)</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>(6,536,735)</td></tr></table>

In accordance with the “MOF’s Considerations on Recognition of Goodwill in China Everbright Bank’s

Acquisition of Former China Investment Bank" (Cai Jin [2001] No. 20) issued in 2001, goodwill is amortised over 20 years from the date of the acquisition and the amortisation is accounted for on a retrospective basis.

Differences between the carrying amount and fair value of the net assets of the former CIB mainly resulted from the provision made for doubtful accounts of the former CIB are recognised as goodwill. According to (Cai Shui [2002] No. 198) “Notice on Tax Issues regarding Write-Off and Disposal of Non-performing Assets of China Everbright Bank” promulgated by the MOF and State Administration of Taxation (Note V. 45), the balance of the relevant provision for doubtful accounts is deductible when the Bank writes off these doubtful accounts in subsequent years. Thus, there is a significant deductible temporary difference, which is recognised as deferred tax assets.

<div style="text-align: center;">Capital surplus</div>



<table border=1 style='margin: auto; word-wrap: break-word;'><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'></td><td colspan="2">2003</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Balance, at 1 January</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>2,293,682</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'></td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Add: Interest payable waived by China Development Bank</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>417,621</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'></td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Add: Foreign currency translation differences</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>1,973</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'></td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Balance, at 31 December</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>2,713,276</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'></td></tr></table>

According to the notice (Cai Jin [2002] No. 186) issued by MOF in 2003, "The Approval of the Issue of Rmb5 billion placement to China Everbright Bank from CDB", the interest expense owed by the Bank to CDB is waived based on the approval by the MOF. As a result, the Bank transferred the interest payable of Rmb418 million to capital surplus in 2003.

In accordance with the reply from the State Administration of Taxation issued on 30 July 2003 "Tax Issues Relating to Relieve of China Everbright Bank's Interest Payables to CDB", the Bank's waived interest payable to CDB can be exempted from income tax payment. Therefore, the Bank does not need to pay for income tax arising from the aforementioned capital surplus recognised.

#### Surplus reserve


<table border=1 style='margin: auto; word-wrap: break-word;'><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'></td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Statutory surplus reserve</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Statutory public welfare fund</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Discretionary surplus reserve</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Total</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>1 January 2003</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>386,207</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>372,632</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>1,092,468</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>1,851,307</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Provision for the year</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>43,340</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>21,670</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>-</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>65,010</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>31 December 2003</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>429,547</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>394,302</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>1,092,468</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>1,916,317</td></tr></table>

According to the Company Law of the People's Republic of China, the Bank's Articles of Association and upon the approval of the Board of Directors, the Bank is required to transfer 10% of its profit after taxation (after compensating prior years' losses) to its statutory surplus reserve until the fund reaches 50% of its registered capital. Statutory surplus reserve can be used to compensate losses or increase capital, but the remaining balance cannot be less than 25% of the registered capital. According to the Board of Directors' resolution dated 9 April 2004, the Bank provided statutory surplus reserve of Rmb43.34 million (2002: Rmb27.158 million), which represents 10% of net profit of 2003.

Statutory public welfare fund is provided at 5%-10% of net profit (after compensating prior year's losses). It can only be used for provision of collective benefits and facilities to the Bank's employees and is not distributable. According to the Board of Directors' resolution dated 9 April 2004, the Bank provided statutory public welfare fund of Rmb21.67 million (2002: 5%, Rmb13.579 million), which represents 5% of net profit of 2003.

The Bank can provide for discretionary surplus reserve when it is proposed by the Board of Directors and approved by the shareholders' general meeting,. Discretionary surplus reserve can be used to compensate prior years' losses or increase capital after proper approval. According to the Board of Directors' resolution dated on 9 April 2004, the Bank did not provide for any discretionary surplus reserve for 2003 (2002: Nil).

Pursuant to the “Company Law of the People’s Republic of China” and the Articles of Association of the Bank, the Bank makes appropriations from the net profit determined under the PRC accounting standards according to the following sequence:

(a) To set off accumulated losses brought forward from prior years;

(b) To provide for statutory surplus reserve fund at 10% of net profit and statutory public welfare fund at 5% to 10% of net profit;

(c) To provide for discretionary surplus reserve fund in accordance with the resolution of the shareholders' meeting;

(d) To pay dividends.

Pursuant to the notice issued by the MOF (Cai Jin [2001] No. 127) “Notice on Administrative Policy for the Making Provision and Write-Off of Doubtful Accounts”, provision should be made in line with the risk levels of the respective financial assets before 2005. Unless approval has been obtained from relevant supervisory authorities, no profit appropriation is allowed unless adequate provision has been made for all financial assets concerned.

Pursuant to a resolution of the shareholders' general meeting on 11 July 2003, a cash dividend of Rmb0.04 per share totalling Rmb329 million was declared and paid for the year 2002 to the 8,216,890,000 outstanding shares. Such resolution has been reported to PBOC.

Income tax


<table border=1 style='margin: auto; word-wrap: break-word;'><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'></td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>2003</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>2002</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Reversal of deferred tax (Note V. 23)</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>381,681</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>340,078</td></tr></table>

Pursuant to the notice issued by the MOF and the State Administration of Taxation (Cai Shui [2002] No. 198) "Notice on Tax Issues regarding Write-Off and Disposal of Non-performing Assets of China Everbright Bank" in 2002, the Bank adopted the following tax treatments when writing off and disposing non-performing assets:

During the period from 2001 to 2009, the Bank should use the specific provision of Rmb14.4 billion made for the non-performing assets transferred from former CIB to set off the doubtful loans and non-performing assets arising from the former CIB.

During the period mentioned above, the actual losses incurred from the non-performing assets of the former CIB can be directly deducted from the Bank’s taxable income upon obtaining the approval from relevant tax authorities.

The reconciliation of statutory income tax to the actual income tax expense is as follows:


<table border=1 style='margin: auto; word-wrap: break-word;'><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'></td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>2003</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>2002</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Profit before tax</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>815,087</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>611,657</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Provision for income tax calculation at statutory rate of 33%</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>268,978</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>201,847</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Exempted interest income on government bonds</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>(475,028)</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>(331,153)</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Non-deductible salaries, welfare, entertainment and other expenses</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>587,731</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>469,384</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Income tax expenses</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>381,681</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>340,078</td></tr></table>

In 2003, the Bank wrote off doubtful accounts of Rmb2.866 billion (2002: Rmb2.382 billion). According to the approval of relevant tax authorities, the Bank netted off the income tax payable for the current year against the related deferred tax debits, to the extent that the current income tax that would otherwise be payable.

All interest income and expenses on loans and deposits with related parties are determined at prevailing commercial interest rates.

(a) As at 31 December 2003 and 2002, the loan granted to China Everbright amounting to Rmb2.164 billion was guaranteed by Qing Dao Jing Tai Properties Co., Ltd. to which China Everbright has pledged its 24.16% share ownership of the Bank.

As at 31 December 2003, loans amounting to Rmb21.8 million (2002: Rmb89 million) granted by the Bank to third parties were guaranteed by China Everbright.

As at 31 December 2003, guarantees issued by the Bank in favor of certain shareholders amounted to approximately Rmb2.4 billion (2002: Rmb2.605 billion). Included in this amount was a guarantee of Rmb2.1 billion (2002: Rmb2.1 billion) for financial bonds issued by China Everbright on 26 June 1997 with an interest rate at 9.18% per annum. As at 31 December 2003, the bonds have been overdue for 42 months since China Everbright failed to honour the redemption of the bonds upon maturity. The defaulted principal and interest concerned amounted to Rmb2.678 billion (2002: Rmb2.678 billion). According to the notices issued by the PBOC (Yin Fa [2002] No. 64-67) "Notices Regarding the Treatment of Certain Debts of China Everbright Group", repayments from China Everbright have been postponed and the repayment schedule will be stipulated by the PBOC in due course. Management of the Bank is of the opinion that no liability would crystallise for the Bank on the financial bonds as a result of the guarantee.

The significant transactions between the Bank, China Everbright and related companies in 2003 included:

Extension of two foreign currency placement contracts with Everbright Finance Corporation amounting to US$146 million and HK$162 million respectively on 31 August 2003.

In January 2003, China Everbright Securities repaid the placement from the Bank amounting to Rmb923 million. On 30 and 31 December, 2003, China Everbright Securities borrowed money from the Bank amounting to Rmb500 million.

## X CONTINGENCIES

Except for items mentioned in Note VII, VIII and IX, as at 31 December 2003, the Bank had been involved in a number of litigation cases with other financial and non-financial institutions as a result of normal banking businesses and its acquisition of former CIB. The outcomes of these cases have not been determined yet. In addition, the Bank considers the disclosure of detailed information of these cases may bring adverse impacts to the final results. The Bank does not anticipate that these legal lawsuits would cause significant losses to the Bank.

## XI CAPITAL ADEQUACY RATIOS

The capital adequacy ratio and core capital adequacy ratio are calculated based on the notice issued by the PBOC (Yin Fa [1996] No. 450) “Notice on the Management, Supervision Guidance and Review of the Ratios of Assets to Liabilities of Commercial Banks” and are as follows:

## XII SUBSEQUENT EVENT

Pursuant to an extraordinary shareholders' meeting on 17 February 2004, the Bank plans to issue secondary debts in 2004 with an amount of no more than Rmb10 billion.

## XIII COMPARATIVE FIGURES

Certain prior year comparative figures in the financial statements have been reclassified to conform with the current year presentation.

## 2003年度大事记

### 1月

1月8日 中国光大银行、招商银行、中国建设银行北京分行、法国兴业（亚洲）有限公司等在香港为京泰实业（集团）公司举行1.8亿美元银团贷款签字仪式。

1月16日 本行2003年临时股东大会、第二届董事会第十次会议、第二届监事会第八次会议在北京召开，董事长王明权、副董事长兼行长王川、监事会主席陈铁林等出席会议。

1月21日－23日 本行在上海召开2003年工作会议。会议提出，本行2003年的经营管理工作要以党的十六大精神为指引，全面实践“三个代表”重要思想，进一步突出以发展为主题，以效益为中心，以质量为基础的经营指导思想，全面加大市场营销、改革创新、增收节支、规范化管理和队伍建设工作力度，努力防范化解风险，扎实做好上市的各项基础性工作，促进各项业务持续快速健康发展，为建设精品银行奠定坚实的基础。光大银行董事长王明权，光大银行行长王川在会议上做重要讲话。

1月22日 经中国人民银行和中国证券监督管理委员会批准同意，本行获得代理开放式基金业务资格。

### 2月

2月11日 经中国人民银行批准，本行获得合格境外机构投资者境内证券投资（QFII）托管业务资格。

2月18日 王川行长等出席合肥分行揭牌仪式。

2月25日 本行托管和代销的第一只基金——中融融华债券型基金正式发行。

### 3月

3月13—14日 中国光大银行2003年度私人业务工作会议在合肥召开。

3月20—21日 中国光大银行2003年度公司暨国际业务工作会议在南京召开。

3月17—19日 中国光大银行2003年度资产保全工作会议在海口召开。

3月31日 光大银行总行在京举办首期反洗钱工作培训班。

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="imgs/img_in_image_box_194_252_1190_518.jpg" alt="Image" width="83%" /></div>


### 4月

4月11日 中国光大银行代理销售中融融华债券型基金总销售量达22.88亿元，这标志着我行代销首只基金工作取得了圆满成功，是光大银行在中间业务领域取得的又一显著成果。

4月15日 中国光大银行2003年一季度分（支）行长例会在北京召开。会议对全行一季度经营情况进行了通报与分析，对下一阶段工作进行了部署与安排。王明权董事长、王川行长分别在会议上作重要讲话。

4月26日－29日 董事长王明权，党委书记、行长王川，党委副书记武青，工会工作委员会主任白元勤等分别深入部分分（支）行看望一线坚守岗位的员工，指导检查“非典”预防工作。

### 5月

5月4日 “五四”青年节，团中央、全国青年文明号活动组委会新命名本行郑州分行营业部为2002年度全国青年文明号，并继续认定去年已获命名的济南分行舜耕支行、黑龙江分行开发区支行为2002年度全国青年文明号。

5月8日 带着总行机关与总行营业部员工深情厚谊的10.8万余元捐款汇往北京市接受捐赠事务管理中心专户，表达了光大银行员工对北京市防治“非典”工作的支持。

### 6月

6月1日 总行开展“95595服务质量月”活动。

6月9日－13日 王川行长一行赴福州分行、厦门分行考察调研，在考察调研中，王川行长就市场定位、风险防范、业务拓展、金融创新、队伍建设等问题作重要讲话。

6月18日—19日 济南分行分别与山东大学、中国重汽集团举行合作协议签字仪式，总行副行长单建保出席仪式。

6月30日 本行资产总额达3876亿元；贷款余额达2423亿元；一般存款达3190亿元，首

次突破了3000亿元大关；在消化部分历史遗留问题后，全行实现账面利润同比增加2.11亿元，主要经营指标实现了时间过半、任务过半，取得了规模、质量与效益的同步快速增长。

## 7月

7月10日 光大银行工会工作委员会举行第一次工作会议，党委书记、行长王川到会做重要讲话，工会工作委员会主任白元勤主持会议。

7月10日 光大银行台州支行正式开业，总行李子卿副行长出席开业仪式并与员工座谈。

7月11日 本行在北京召开第七次股东大会和第三届董事会、监事会第一次会议，审议通过了《中国光大银行2002年度董事会工作报告》、《中国光大银行2002年度监事会工作报告》、《关于中国光大银行2002年度财务决算和2003年度财务预算的报告》、《中国光大银行2002年度利润分配预案》和《关于授权董事会根据银监会的意见对本行章程若干条款进行修改的议案》等；选举产生了中国光大银行第三届董事会和监事会的董事和监事成员，选举王明权同志为董事长、王川同志为副董事长、陈铁林同志为监事会主席。

7月17–18日 光大银行2003年年中全国分（支）行行长会议在北京召开。会议对上半年的工作进行了认真总结，对下半年的工作进行了安排，并提出了具体的工作重点与措施。

7月24日 光大银行启动2003年首次大型公司业务客户经理多媒体培训工作。

## 8月

8月1日 经国家外汇管理局审核批准，光大银行在全国范围内开通境内居民个人购汇业务。

8月5日 “珍爱健康，享受阳光”——中国光大银行阳光国际旅行卡首发式在北京光大大厦举行。王川行长，VISA国际组织中国区总经理、亚太区副总裁熊安平到会讲话并为阳光国际旅行卡揭幕。

8月13日 总行召开机关全体员工动员大会，启动了全系统以“整顿工作纪律，整顿工作作风，提高工作效率，提高经营管理水平”为主题的“双整顿、双提高”活动。

8月21日 济南分行举行“光大银行企业理财产品展示会”，100多家重点客户单位、400多人参加了展示会。

8月22日 光大银行ISO9001质量体系认证工作项目组成立，ISO9001质量体系认证工作正式启动。

8月25日－29日 王明权董事长率团出访亚行，与亚行金立群副行长、巨奎林执董和私营局ROBERT BESTANI局长等高层官员会晤，磋商继续投资与合作事宜。随后，代表团一行考察了韩国比较有代表性的三家代理行——韩亚银行、外换银行和产业银行。

<div style="text-align: center;"><img src="imgs/img_in_image_box_198_256_1190_519.jpg" alt="Image" width="83%" /></div>


8月27日-28日 王川行长一行赴沈阳分行、大连分行考察调研。王川行长认真听取了两行领导班子的工作汇报，在中层干部会议上作了重要指示，并对两行正在进行的“双整顿、双提高”工作进行了巡视、指导。

## 9月

9月2日—5日 “金卡工程（银行卡）十年成就展”在北京展览馆隆重举办，光大银行设立展位成功参展。

9月8日-11日 光大银行2003年计财会计工作会议在大连召开，李杰副行长对1-8月份经营管理工作进行通报，就计财会计工作提出进一步要求。

9月28日 总行机关成功举办了“中国光大银行机动车专场拍卖会”，标志着总行本部的车改工作按计划顺利完成。

9月29日 光大银行在宜昌分别与中国长江三峡工程开发总公司和中国长江电力股份有限公司签订长期合作框架协议。总行党委副书记、副行长武青出席签字仪式。

## 10月

10月8日 历时两个月的光大银行“理财大本营”网上推广活动抽奖揭晓，4个一等奖、8个二等奖、2000个幸运奖各有得主。

10月9日 单建保副行长在广州主持召开了南部地区审批中心试点方案落实协调会。20日，信贷组织架构改革试点方案及其配套措施在华南地区正式启动，南部地区审批中心和辖内各分（支）行信贷控制部以及放款中心正式运行。

10月15日 光大银行第三届董事会第二次会议、第三届监事会第二次会议在上海召开，董事长王明权、副董事长兼行长王川、监事会主席陈铁林等出席了会议。

10月16日－17日 光大银行2003年三季度分（支）行行长例会在杭州召开。会议认真总结了前三季度经营管理工作，全面部署安排第四季度和今后一个时期的工作重点，提出要正

确分析并顺应形势发展和政策变化，充分做好当前各项重点工作，确保圆满完成全年各项工作任务。董事长王明权、行长王川、监事会主席陈铁林出席会议并分别做重要讲话。

10月20日 总行在北京召开了项目动员大会，光大银行新一代核心业务和管理会计系统项目开发正式启动。

## 11月

11月3日 本行保理系统在全辖正式上线运行。

11月4日 本行综合办公管理系统正式启动。

11月5日 光大集团监事会主席蔡鄂生到太原分行视察工作。

11月6日—7日 王川行长在珠海出席世界经济发展宣言大会。

11月13–14日 王明权董事长召集南京、苏州、合肥、武汉、长沙、重庆等六家分行的负责人在合肥召开调研座谈会。总行党委副书记、副行长武青、总行副行级董事会秘书陈元生参加了会议。

11月15日 “中国光大银行海南地区资产处置拍卖会”在海南举行。

## 12月

12月4日-5日 王明权董事长在广州召集广州、深圳、昆明、厦门、南宁、海口6家分（支）行负责人召开调研座谈会，总行党委副书记、副行长武青，副行级董事会秘书陈元生参加了会议。

12月5日 JP摩根大通银行向光大银行颁发了2002年清算质量认证奖。这是继2000年、2001年后我行第三次获得该奖项，也是国内同业中唯一连续三次获奖的银行。

12月6日 苏州支行升格分行暨成立5周年、同城分理处升格支行庆典活动隆重举行，总行单建保副行长出席庆典活动。

12月16日 中国光大银行第三届董事会第三次会议、第三届监事会第三次会议在北京召开，中国光大银行董事长王明权、副董事长兼行长王川、监事会主席陈铁林等出席了会议。

# CHRONICLE OF EVENTS FOR 2003

#### JANUARY

JAN. 8 China Everbright Bank joined China Merchant Bank, China Construction Bank, Beijing Branch and Socieite Generale (Asia) Ltd. Co. in the signing ceremony of syndicate loan of USD180 million to Jingtai Industrial Co., Ltd. in Hong Kong.

JAN. 16 The Provisional Shareholders' Meeting, the Tenth Meeting of the Second Board of Directors, as well as the Eighth Meeting of the Second Board of Supervisors were held in Beijing, with the participation of Mr. Wang Mingquan, Chairman of the Board of Directors, Mr. Wang Chuan, Vice Chairman of the Board of Directors and Mr. Chen Tielin, Chairman of the Board of the Supervisors.

Jan. 21-Jan. 23 The Working Conference for 2003 was convened in Shanghai. The main tasks of operation and management for 2003 were made at the meeting: under the guidelines set forth by the Sixteenth CPC National Congress as well as the key concept of "Three Representations", the Bank shall thoroughly enhance its capability in marketing, reform and innovation, budget control, standardized management and faculty team build-up, as well as make more efforts in risk mitigation by placing the theme of development at the top priority, profits at the core and quality as the basis in a bid to make good fundamental preparation for the Bank's IPO, promote a sound and rapid development of all business sectors, and lay a solid foundation for building the Bank in to a model bank in China. Mr. Wang Mingquan, Chairman of the Bank, and Mr. Wang Chuan, President of the Bank delivered important speeches respectively during the meeting.

Jan. 22 Upon the approval of PBOC and CSRC, the Bank gained the qualification of conducting open-end fund custody and distribution services.

## FEBRUARY

Feb. 11 Upon the approval of PBOC, the Bank obtained the qualification of providing custody service for QRII to invest in domestic securities.

Feb.18 President Wang Chuan attended the Opening Ceremony of Hefei Branch.

Feb.25 Zhongrong Ronghua Bond Fund, the first investment fund which the Bank acted as distribution agent and custodian, was formally issued to the public.

##### MARCH

Mar.13-Mar.14 Working Meeting of Retail Banking Business for 2003 was held in Hefei.

Mar.20-Mar.21 Working Meeting of Corporate Banking Business for 2003 was held in Nanjing.

Mar.17-Mar.19 Working Meeting of Special Loan Administration for 2003 was held in Haikou.

Mar.31 The first session of Anti-Money Laundering training program was held by Head Office in Beijing.

### APRIL

Apr.11 The total sale volume of Zhongrong Ronghua Bond Fund distributed by the Bank amounted to RMB2.288 billion yuan, which represented a complete success for the Bank maiden work in fund custody businesses, and another achievement in its promotion of fee-based business.

Apr.15 The first-quarter routine meeting of branch (sub-branch) principals was held in Beijing. Reports as well as analysis of the operating performance for the first quarter were made and tasks for the next period were mapped out and designated at the meeting. Chairman Wang Mingquan and President Wang Chuan delivered important speeches respectively during the meeting.

Apr.26-Apr.29 Mr. Wang Mingquan, Chairman of Board of Directors, Mr. Wang Chuan, President of the Bank, Mr. Wu Qing, Deputy Secretar of Party Committee of the Bank and Mr. Bai Yuanqin, Head of Working Committee of CEB Labor Union went to various branches (sub-branches) to conduct investigation of SARS prevention and visit staff members at the frontier who stuck to their posts during the outbreak of SARS epidemic.

### MAY

May 4 At the time of May Fourth Youth Day, Center of China Communist Youth League together with the Organization Committee of National Prize for Excellent Youth Unit added the banking department of the Bank's Zhengzhou Branch as a winner of the National Excellent Youth Unit prize for year 2002. The Bank's Shungeng Sub-branch of Jinan Branch and Development Zone Sub-branch of Heilongjiang Branch, which had won this prize in year 2001, continued to preserve this title for year 2002.

May 8 A cordial donation of over RMB108 thousand yuan from all staff members of Head Office and Banking Department of the Bank was made to the special account opened by Beijing Donation Receipt and Management Center, which shows the employee's support and assistant to Beijing Municipality Government for their prevention work against SARS.

#### JUNE

Jun. 1 The program, entitled with "95595 Service Quality Month" and aiming at quality improvement, was initiated by Head Office of the Bank.

Jun. 9-Jun.13 A delegation headed by President Wang Chuan conducted investigations in Fuzhou and Xiamen Branch. President Wang Chuan concluded some key issues of the Bank in his speeches including market positioning, risk management and mitigation, financial innovation, and faculty team build-up.

Jun.18-Jun.19 Mr. Shan Jiangbao, Executive Vice President of the Bank, attended the signing ceremony of Jinan Branch for its Cooperation Agreements with Shandong University and China National Heavy Duty Truck Group Co., Ltd. respectively.

Jun.30 At the time when the first half year was about to end, the Bank achieved rapid development in size, quality, and profitability with total assets reaching RMB387.6 billion yuan., total outstanding loans amounting to RMB242.3 billion yuan., corporate and savings deposits being increased to RMB319 billion which is the breakthrough of RMB300 billion yuan, and pretaxation profit being increased by RMB211 million over that

of the corresponding period of the previous year.

#### JULY

Jul.10 The First Working Meeting of Working Committee of Labor Union of the Bank was convened. Mr. Wang Chuan, President as well as Party Secretariat of the Bank delivered speech during the meeting. The meeting was presided by Mr. Bai Yuanqin, Head of Working Committee of the Bank's Labor Union.

Jul.10 Li Ziqing, Executive Vice President of the Bank, attended the opening ceremony of Taizhou Subbranch and held panel discussions with various staff members.

Jul.11 The Seventh General Shareholders' Meeting, the First Meeting of the Third Board of Directors and the First Meeting of the Third Board of Supervisors were held respectively. Proposals including Working Report of Board of Directors for 2002, Working Report of Board of Supervisors for 2002, Report on Financial Settlement for 2002 and Financial Budget for 2003, Draft Proposal for Profit Distribution of 2002, Proposal on Authorization to Board of Directors to Make Revisions to Some Articles of the Bank's Articles of Association Based on CBRC's Comments were examined and approved during the meeting. The meetings also elected the members of the Bank's Third session of Board of Directors and Board of Supervisors with Mr. Wang Mingquan as the Chairman of Board of Directors, Mr. Wang Chuan as Vice Chairman of Board of Directors, and Mr. Chen Tielin as Chairman of Board of Supervisors.

Jul.17-Jul.18 Mid-Year Meeting of 2003 of branch (sub-branch) principal was held in Beijing, which made a careful review of the Bank's business performance for the first half and set working tasks for the second half of year 2003 with detailed and specific highlights and measures.

Jul.24 The Bank, for the first time, initiated training program via multimedia to its account managers for large corporations.

#### AUGUST

Aug.1 Upon the approval of SAFE, the Bank started to provide nationwide RMB to foreign exchange purchase services for domestic individual customers.

Aug.5 The issuance ceremony for the Bank's international travel SunCard was held in Everbright Building, Beijing with the logo of "Cherish Life and Enjoy Sunshine". President Wang Chuan and Mr. Xiong Anping, General Manager of VISA China Region and Vice Director of VISA Asia Pacific Region, respectively delivered speeches and jointly inaugurated the issuance of the Card.

Aug.13 Head Office launched the "dual consolidation and dual improvement" program aiming at consolidating consolidating working discipline, consolidating working professionalism, improving efficiency and improving business management and operation.

Aug.21 The Bank's Jinan Branch held Exhibition on Property Management Products with the participation of more than 400 delegates from over 100 quality customers of the Bank.

Aug.22 The task force for ISO9001 Certification Project was established, which indicated the formal initiation for the ISO9001 Certification process.

Aug.25-Aug.29 A delegation headed by Chairman Wang Mingquan visited Asian Development Bank and held a meeting with Mr. Jin Liqun, Vice President of ADB, Mr. Ju Kuilin, Executive Director and Mr. Robert Bestani, Director General of Private Sector Operations Department (PSOD), to discuss ADB's continued investment in the Bank and further cooperation between the two banks. The delegation also visited three tcorrespondent banks in Korea, namely Hana Bank, Korea Exchange Bank and Korea Development Bank.

Aug.27-Aug.28 President Wang Chuan led an inspection delegation in Shenyang Branch and Dalian Branch. After careful listening of work report submitted by principals of the two branches, President Wang gave important instructions and guidelines during meetings participated by all middle-level managers from the two branches. The delegation also conducted inspections upon the implementation status of "dual consolidation and dual improvement" in the two branches.

## SEPTEMBER

Sep.2-Sep.5 The Bank participated and set up an exhibition stand in "Achievement Exhibition of Gold-Card (Banking Card) Project for the Past Decade" in Beijing Exhibition Hall.

Sep.8-Sep.11 Meeting of the Bank's planning and finance management for year 2003 was held in Dalian. During the meeting, Ms. Li Jie, Executive Vice President of the Bank, delivered them report on the general operation and management for the first eight months in the aspect of financial management and made further instructions and requirements for the Bank's future planning and finance management.

Sep.28 As an important part of the Bank's "Automobile Reform", an "CEB Automobile Auction" was successfully held at Head Office, representing the smooth and successful completion of the "Automobile Reform" at the level of Head office of the Bank.

Sep.29 The Bank signed the Master Agreements for long-term cooperation with China Three Gorges Project Cooperation and China Yangtze Power Co., Ltd. Respectively. Mr. Wu Qing, Deputy Secretariat and Deputy President, participated in the signing ceremony.

#### OCTOBER

Oct.8 The result of lucky draw program for the two-months promotion of the on-line "Property Management Camp" was publicized, granting four Gold Metal Prizes, eight Silver Metal Prizes and 2000 Luckiful Prizes.

Oct.9 Mr. Shan Jianbao, Executive Vice President of the Bank, convened the coordination meeting in Guangzhou for the implementation of South China Credit Approval Center pioneer program. On October 20, the pioneer program for the reform of credit structure of the Bank as well as other corresponding measures were officially initiated in South China, after which South China Credit Approval Center, and Credit Control Department with Loan Releasing Centre at various branches in the region were formerly set up and put in to actual operations.

Oct.15 The Second Meeting of the Third Board of Directors and the Second Meeting of the Third Board of Supervisors were held in Shanghai with the participation of Chairman Wang Mingquan, Vice Chairman and President Wang Chuan and Chairman Chen Tielin of Board of Supervisors.

Oct.16-Oct.17 The third-quarter routine meeting of branch (sub-branch) principals was held in Hangzhou.

which reviewed business operations and management for the first three quarters, deployed the major task for the Fourth Quarter and the next period, and required a thorough fulfillment of major works and realization of all business targets in light of the policy change and market evolution Mr. Wang Mingquan, Chairman of Board of Directors, President Wang Chuan, and Mr. Chen Tielin, Chairman of Board of Supervisors, attended the meeting and delivered important speeches during the meeting respectively.

Oct. 20 The mobilization meeting was held in Beijing, after which the Project of Core Banking Business System and Management Accounting System were officially initiated.

### NOVEMBER

Nov.3 Factoring System was officially put into live production in Head Office and all branches (sub-branches).

Nov.4 Office Management System was officially implemented.

Nov.5 Mr. Cai Esheng, Chairman of Board of Supervisors of China Everbright Group Ltd. appointed by CBRC, inspected Taiyuan Branch.

Nov.6-Nov.7 President Wang Chuan attended The World Economic Development Declaration Summit in Zhu Hai.

Nov.13-Nov.14 Chairman Wang Mingquan convened an investigative meeting in Heifei with participation of principals Nanjing Branch, Suzhou Branch, He Fei Branch, Wuhan Branch, Changsha Branch and Chongqing Branch Mr. Wu Qing, Deputy Party Secretariat and Deputy President, and Mr. Chen Yuansheng, Secretary to Board of Directors (EVP ranking) also attended the meeting.

Nov.15 "The Aunction for CEB Non-performing Assets in Hainan Province" was held in Hainan.

#### DECEMBER

Dec.4-Dec.5 Chairman Wang Mingquan convened an investigative meeting with participation of principals from Guangzhou Branch, Shenzhen Branch, Kunming Branch, Xiamen Branch, Nanning Branch and Haikou Branch. Deputy Party Secretariat and Deputy President and Mr. Chen Yuansheng, Secretary to Board of Directors (EVP ranking) also attended in the meetings.

Dec.5 JP Morgan Chase Bank issued its Clearing Quality Certification Award of 2002 to the Bank, with which the Bank becomes the only bank in China that won this award for three consecutive years.

Dec.6 The celebration for 5th Anniversary of the Upgrading of Suzhou Branch and the Upgrading of Banking Offices to Sub-branches was held with the participation of EVP Shan Jianbao.

Dec.16 The Third Meeting of the Third Board of Directors and the Third Meeting of the Third Board of Supervisors were held in Beijing with the participation of Chairman Wang Mingquan, Vice Chairman and President Wang Chuan and Chairman of Board of Supervisors Chen Tielin.
